obey the subcriticality condition (
2.22
) for some
\updelta>0
. Then the
\partial_{t}
-derivative of the total low-frequency energy quantity obeys the following bound, uniformly in
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
and
0<\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1
:

\left|t\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)\right|\lesssim\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t).

(6.9)

There exists some
\mathring{C}_{low}
, depending only on the background Kasner spacetime, such that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
and
0<\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1
, one has

\mathring{C}_{low}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)\leq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*})\leq\mathring{C}_{low}\,\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t).

(6.10)

Proof (assuming Proposition
6.4
).

The derivative estimate (
6.9
) follows immediately from Proposition
6.4
and the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)
upon insertion of the expressions
\mathring{g}_{ij}(t_{\lambda*})=t_{\lambda*}^{2p_{\underline{i}}}\delta_{\underline{i}j}
and
\mathring{g}^{ij}(t_{\lambda*})=t_{\lambda*}^{-2p_{\underline{i}}}\delta^{\underline{i}j}
.

To deduce (
6.10
), we make the simple observation that

\int^{t_{\lambda*}}_{0}\left(\frac{s}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\,\frac{ds}{s}\leq\updelta^{-1},

thus applying Grönwall’s inequality to (
6.9
) in both the forwards and backwards directions yields that for an
0<t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{\lambda*}
, we have (where
C
is the implied constant in (
6.9
)):

\exp( - C \updelta^{-1} )\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{1})\leq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{2})\leq\exp( C \updelta^{-1} )\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{1}).

The energy estimate (
6.10
) then follows upon choosing
t_{2}=t_{\lambda*}
and
\mathring{C}_{low}=C\updelta^{-1}
.
∎

In Section
6.2
, we prove Proposition
6.4
. We shall also use Proposition
6.4
to produce limiting quantities
{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda},(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda})
in Proposition
6.5
.

6.2
Derivative estimates using the subcriticality condition

In order to prove the energy estimate in Proposition
6.2
, we will have to estimate the powers of
t
arising in the evolution equations of Lemma
6.1
. The first step will be to estimate the integral quantity
G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Consider the tensor
G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}
defined in (
6.1
). It satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\left|G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}(t;t_{\lambda*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\mathring{g}_{ip}(t)\mathring{g}^{jq}(t),\mathring{g}_{ip}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}^{jq}(t_{\lambda*})\right\}\cdot\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2p_{\underline{i}}-2p_{\underline{j}}},1\right\}\cdot t_{\lambda*}^{2p_{\underline{i}}-2p_{\underline{j}}}\cdot\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\cdot\delta_{\underline{i}p}\,\delta^{\underline{j}q}.

(6.11)

Proof.

One simply notes that for any
i,p,j,q
, the quantity
\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)
is either monotonically increasing or monotonically decreasing in
s\in[t,t_{\lambda*}]
, depending on whether
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
or
p_{i}\geq p_{j}
. Therefore

G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}=\int^{t_{\lambda*}}_{t}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)\frac{ds}{s}\leq\max\left\{\mathring{g}_{ip}(t)\mathring{g}^{jq}(t),\mathring{g}_{ip}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}^{jq}(t_{\lambda*})\right\}\cdot\int^{t_{\lambda*}}_{t}\frac{ds}{s},

from which the first line in (
6.3
) follows immediately. The second line follows upon inserting (
2.14
).
∎

We now use this lemma, along with the evolution equations in Lemma
6.1
to show that the time derivatives of our matter and metric quantities are integrable towards
t=0
. The subcriticality condition (
2.22
) will be crucial in order to produce a power of
t
that is integrable.

Proposition 6.4
.

For
({({\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda})
as in Proposition
6.2
, define
{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}
as in (
6.1
), (
6.2
). Then given the subcriticality condition (
2.22
) on the background Kasner exponents, we have the following derivative estimates uniformly in
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
and
0<\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1
.

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}|t\partial_{t}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|+t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}|t\partial_{t}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|+|t\partial_{t}\uppsi_{\lambda}|+|t\partial_{t}\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}|\lesssim\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}(t).

(6.12)

Proof.

Let us start with
|t\partial_{t}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|
. We will bound this by grouping the terms in (
6.3
) as follows:

\displaystyle|t\partial_{t}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|

\displaystyle=-t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{b}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{j}}+t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ja}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{b}}

(6.13a)

\displaystyle\quad+G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}-G_{ap}^{\phantom{ap}jq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{b}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}-G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}bq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ja}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}

(6.13b)

\displaystyle\quad-t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ja}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{a}(\tr\tilde{\Upsilon})+t^{2}\mathring{g}^{jk}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{k}\upnu_{\lambda}-(t{\mathring{k}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}})\upnu_{\lambda}.

(6.13c)

We shall estimate the terms occuring in (
6.13a
)–(
6.13c
) in terms of the low-frequency energy
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)
. By the definition of the low-frequency energy, we have that

\left|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\right|\lesssim t_{\lambda*}^{p_{i}-p_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2},\qquad\left|{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\right|\lesssim t_{\lambda*}^{p_{i}-p_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2},\qquad\left|\tr\tilde{\Upupsilon}\right|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.14)

On the other hand, we estimate
\lambda_{i}
using (
3.3
) and
0<\tau^{2}=\sum_{i}t^{2-2p_{i}}\lambda_{i}^{2}\leq 1
as follows

|\lambda_{i}|\leq t_{\lambda*}^{p_{i}-1}.

(6.15)

Using these estimates and (
2.14
), it is straightforward to then estimate (
6.13a
) in the following manner.

\displaystyle t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\cdot|\text{\eqref{eq:upkappa_low_a}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{a=1}^{D}\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{a}}\cdot\left[t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\left|{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\right|+t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{a}+p_{j}}\left|{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{j}}\right|+t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{a}}\left|{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{a}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{a=1,\ldots,D}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{a}}\right\}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.16)

For the second line (
6.13b
), we first exploit a crucial cancellation in the first and third terms of (
6.13b
) when
b=j
. In particular, from the cancellation and (
6.3
) one has

|G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}-G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}bq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ja}|\lesssim\max_{i,j\neq b}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{i}-2p_{j}-2p_{b}}\right\}\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\cdot t_{\lambda*}^{2-p_{a}-p_{b}-p_{q}+p_{p}}.

To get this estimate, there are really two cases depending on whether
p_{i}>p_{j}
or
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
; mnote that the case where
p_{i}>p_{j}
in (
6.3
) is covered by allowing
i=j
in the above maximum.
Estimating the second term in (
6.13b
) using (
6.3
) is more straightforward – in that the power of
t
ends up being
t^{2-2p_{a}}
or
t^{2-2p_{j}}
rather than featuring more complicated linear combinations of Kasner exponents – and one eventually yields

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\cdot|\text{\eqref{eq:upkappa_low_b}}|\lesssim\max_{j\neq b}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{i}-2p_{j}-2p_{\underline{b}}}\right\}\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.17)

To deal with (
6.13c
), the
\tr\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda}
term is straightforward, while for the
\upnu_{\lambda}
term we may use the elliptic equation (
4.42
) to get the following preliminary estimate, recalling that now
\tau^{2}\leq 1
:

\displaystyle|\upnu_{\lambda}|

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}\left|\tr\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda}\right|+t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{a}\left|{(\tilde{\Upupsilon_{\lambda}})}_{{b}}^{\phantom{{b}}{i}}\right|+G_{bp}^{\phantom{bp}iq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{a}\left|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{a=1,\ldots,D}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{a}}\right\}\left(1+\log\left(\frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t}\right)\right)\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.18)

As the final term of (
6.13c
) contributes only when
i=j
, and
t{\mathring{k}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
is bounded by
\max p_{i}
, we therefore have

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\cdot|\text{\eqref{eq:upkappa_low_c}}|\lesssim\max_{a=1,\ldots,D}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{a}}\right\}\left(1+\log\left(\frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t}\right)\right)\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.19)

Combining all of (
6.16
), (
6.17
) and (
6.19
), we therefore have

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}|t\partial_{t}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|\lesssim\max_{b\neq c}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.20)

We next move onto
|t\partial_{t}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|
. Using (
6.4
) and the above, we write this as follows:

\displaystyle t\partial_{t}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}

\displaystyle=-G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}js}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{s}}^{\phantom{{s}}{r}}+G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}ja}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{sb}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{s}}^{\phantom{{s}}{r}}+G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}js}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ra}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{s}}^{\phantom{{s}}{b}}

(6.21a)

\displaystyle\;+G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}js}G_{sp}^{\phantom{sp}rq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}-G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}ja}G_{sp}^{\phantom{sp}rq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{sb}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}-G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}js}G_{sp}^{\phantom{sp}bq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ra}\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}

(6.21b)

\displaystyle\;-G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}js}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ra}\lambda_{s}\lambda_{a}(\tr\tilde{\Upsilon})+G_{ir}^{\phantom{ir}js}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{rk}\lambda_{s}\lambda_{k}\upnu_{\lambda}-G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}(t{\mathring{k}}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}})\upnu_{\lambda}+(t{\mathring{k}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}})\upnu_{\lambda}.

(6.21c)

To estimate (
6.21a
), note that there is a cancellation between the first two terms when
a=s
, in a similar fashion to the cancellation in the estimate of (
6.13c
) above. Tracking the powers of
t
and
t_{\lambda*}
carefully, and using (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) as before, one yields

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\cdot|\text{\eqref{eq:upupsilon_low_a}}|\lesssim\max_{a,b\neq c}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.22)

For (
6.21b
), there are two cases depending on whether
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
or
p_{i}\geq p_{j}
. If
p_{i}\leq p_{j}
, there is a cancellation between the first and the second expression when
a=s
while the contribution of the third expression is fine. Alternatively if
p_{i}\geq p_{j}
, there is a cancellation between the first and the third when
b=r
while the contribution of the second is fine. Omitting the details, we end up at the following estimate

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\cdot|\text{\eqref{eq:upupsilon_low_b}}|\lesssim\max_{a,b\neq c}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)^{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.23)

The term involving
\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t})
appears with a square since (
6.21b
) is quadratic in the
G_{ab}^{\phantom{ab}cd}
tensor.

Finally, for (
6.21c
), we use again (
6.18
) and the fact that the third and fourth terms are nonzero if and only if
i=j
. Therefore

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\cdot|\text{\eqref{eq:upupsilon_low_c}}|\lesssim\max_{a=1,\ldots,D}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{a}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)^{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.24)

The square appears here because we must use (
6.3
) on to of (
6.18
) to estimate the third term
G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}(t{\mathring{k}}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}})\upnu_{\lambda}
. Combining the three estimates (
6.21a
), (
6.21b
), (
6.21c
) then yields

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}|t\partial_{t}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|\lesssim\max_{a,b\neq c}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)^{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.25)

(We reiterate that powers of
t
such as
\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{c}}
are included in (
6.25
) upon taking
a=b
in the
\max
.)

For the matter quantities
\uppsi_{\lambda}
and
\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}
, we use (
6.5
) and (
6.6
). Here, it is straightforward to use
(
6.18
)
as well as the trivial bounds from the definition of the low-frequency energy:

|\uppsi_{\lambda}|+|\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

Inserting all these bounds into (
6.5
) and (
6.6
), one deduces

|t\partial_{t}\uppsi_{\lambda}|+|t\partial_{t}\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}|\lesssim\max_{a=1,\ldots,D}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{a}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)^{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

(6.26)

Combining all of (
6.20
), (
6.25
) and (
6.26
), one deduces the inequality.

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}\left(|t\partial_{t}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|+|t\partial_{t}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|\right)+|t\partial_{t}\uppsi_{\lambda}|+|t\partial_{t}\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}|\lesssim\max_{a,b\neq c}\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}}\right\}\left(1+\log( \frac{t_{\lambda*}}{t} )\right)^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}.

At this point that we apply the subcriticality condition (
2.22
). Since, for
b\neq c
, this condition tells us
2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}\geq 2\updelta
, we may apply the simple inequality
x^{\updelta}(1+\log x^{-1})^{2}\lesssim 1
for
x=\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}
, to yield the desired derivative bound (
6.12
).
∎

Remark
.

A more careful study of the proof shows that the only terms which give a contribution of
\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2+2p_{a}-2p_{b}-2p_{c}}
rather than just
\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2-2p_{c}}
are terms that involve the expression

\left(G_{ap}^{\phantom{ap}bq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{cd}-G_{ap}^{\phantom{ap}cq}t^{2}\mathring{g}^{bd}\right)\lambda_{c}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}.

(6.27)

Upon relabelling indices, this arises in (
6.13b
), and in the case that
p_{i}\geq p_{j}
, we also see it in (
6.21b
). (One may use the symmetry constraint
\mathring{g}^{ab}(t_{\lambda*}){\tilde{\Upupsilon}}_{{b}}^{\phantom{{b}}{c}}=\mathring{g}^{cb}(t_{\lambda*}){\tilde{\Upupsilon}}_{{b}}^{\phantom{{b}}{a}}
to reduce to this case.)

Therefore, even if the subcriticality condition (
2.22
) fails, it is possible to close the argument if the expression (
6.27
) can be shown to converge to
0
sufficiently fast as
t\to 0
. Upon expanding out
G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}
in (
6.27
), one may show that (
6.27
) converging to
0
requires (
2.23
); note however to close the argument one must find the rate of convergence in (
2.23
).

6.3
Convergence to a limit

To find the scattering states
({(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\infty},\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})
, one must show that
({\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t),{\tilde{\Upupsilon}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t),\uppsi(t),\tilde{\upvarphi}(t))
converges as
t\to 0
. Conversely, for asymptotic completeness we also need to show that one can prescribe these limits and launch a solution to the system (
4.36
)–(
4.50
). We achieve this in the following proposition.

Proposition 6.5
.

For fixed
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
, and
\mathring{C}_{low}>0
as in Proposition
6.2
, the following hold.

(i)

Let
({({\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda})
be a solution to the system (
4.36
)–(
4.50
) for
0<\tau(t)\leq 1

in CMCTC gauge
. Then there exist constant
(1,1)
tensors
{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and real numbers
(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}
,
(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda}
such that
{({\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)
,
{({\tilde{\Upupsilon}}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)
,
\uppsi_{\lambda}(t)
and
\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}(t)
attain these as limits as
t\to 0
, at the following rate

\displaystyle|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)-{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|+|{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)-{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|\lesssim t_{\lambda*}^{p_{i}-p_{j}}\cdot\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}(t_{\lambda*}),

(6.28)

\displaystyle|\uppsi_{\lambda}(t)-(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}|+|\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}(t)-(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda}|\lesssim\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}(t_{\lambda*}).

(6.29)

Furthermore, one has the following estimate on the limiting quantities:

\mathring{g}^{ac}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(t_{\lambda*})\left[{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}+{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}\right]+(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}+(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}\leq\mathring{C}_{low}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*}).

(ii)

Conversely, let
{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
be constant
(1,1)
tensors and
(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}
and
(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda}
be real numbers, satisfying the
\lambda
-Fourier projections of the asymptotic constraints (
4.25
)–(
4.29
), for
T=t_{\lambda*}
. Then there exists a unique solution
({({\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda})
to the system (
4.36
)–(
4.50
) for
0<\tau(t)\leq 1

in CMCTC gauge
such that the solution obeys the asymptotics (
6.28
)–(
6.29
).

For this solution, one has that

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*})\leq\mathring{C}_{low}\left(\mathring{g}^{ac}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(t_{\lambda*})\left[{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}+{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}\right]+(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}+(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}\right).

Proof.

We first prove (i). This follows straightforwardly upon combining Proposition
6.2
with Proposition
6.4
. More specifically, combining (
6.10
) with (
6.12
) yields that for a solution
({({\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda})
to the system (
4.36
)–(
4.50
) in the range
0<\tau(t)\leq 1
,

t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}|t\partial_{t}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|+t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}|t\partial_{t}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|+|t\partial_{t}\uppsi_{\lambda}|+|t\partial_{t}\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}|\lesssim\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}(t_{\lambda*}).

For
\updelta>0
,
t^{\updelta-1}
is integrable towards
t=0
, thus there is a limit
({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda})\to({((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda},(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda})
as
t\to 0
, and moreover that the estimates (
6.28
) and (
6.29
) are satisfied. The final estimate in (i) then follows straightforwardly from Proposition
6.2
.

Moving onto (ii), we apply the Fuchsian theory of first-order ODEs. In order to do so, we firstly reinterpret Proposition
6.4
as follows. This proposition tells us that solutions
\mathbf{V}=(t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{\underline{i}}+p_{\underline{j}}}{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{\underline{i}}}^{\phantom{{\underline{i}}}{\underline{j}}},t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{\underline{i}}+p_{\underline{j}}}{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{\underline{i}}}^{\phantom{{\underline{i}}}{\underline{j}}},\uppsi_{\lambda},\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda})
to the first-order hyperbolic system (
6.3
)–(
6.6
) coupled to the elliptic equation (
4.42
) satisfy the following Fuchsian ODE:

t\frac{d\mathbf{V}}{dt}=\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}F\left[\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}},\mathbf{V}\right],

where
F
is bounded and continuous in both its variables and linear in
\mathbf{V}
.

Therefore, we may apply Lemma
B.1
(b) to the above ODE, with
A=0
and initial data

\mathbf{v}=(t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{\underline{i}}+p_{\underline{j}}}{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{\underline{i}}}^{\phantom{{\underline{i}}}{\underline{j}}},t_{\lambda*}^{-p_{\underline{i}}+p_{\underline{j}}}{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{\underline{i}}}^{\phantom{{\underline{i}}}{\underline{j}}},(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda},(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda}).

The lemma generates a solution
({({\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda})
to the first-order elliptic hyperbolic system above, which moreover attains the asymptotics (
6.28
) and (
6.29
). Moreover, the solution is unique in the whole domain
0<t\leq t_{\lambda*}
. (Note Lemma
B.1
(b) only gives local existence and uniqueness, but since the system (
6.3
)–(
6.6
) and (
4.42
) are linear one may extend to the whole domain
0<t\leq t_{\lambda*}
.)

So far, the argument (including Proposition
6.4
) has
not
used the constraint equations (
4.45
)–(
4.50
). It remains to recover these constraints for the solution we generated; we leave this to Proposition
C.2
. It is only here that we require that
({((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{((\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda},(\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\infty})_{\lambda})
satisfy the asymptotic constraints (
4.51
)–(
4.55
).
This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

6.4
Connecting the high and low frequency energies

We shall eventually show that in a well-chosen gauge, the high-frequency energy quantity
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(t)
of Definition
5.1
and the low-frequency energy quantity
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)
of Definition
6.1
are comparable. One subtlety is that
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(t)
only has good coercivity properties for
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\geq\mathring{\tau}\geq 1
, while
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)
is defined only for
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1
. To fill in the gap
1\leq\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq\mathring{\tau}
, we rely on the following
mid-frequency energy estimate
. Like the high-frequency energy estimate, this will
always use the CMCSH gauge
(
4.35
).

Definition 6.2
.

The
geometric mid-frequency energy quantity

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\upeta,mid}(t)
, used for
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
and
1\leq\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq\mathring{\tau}
and solutions in the CMCSH gauge, is given by

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\upeta,mid}(t)\coloneqq\mathring{g}^{ac}(t)\mathring{g}_{bd}(t)\,{(\hat{\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{(\hat{\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}+\mathring{g}^{ac}(t)\mathring{g}_{bd}(t)\,{(\hat{\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{(\hat{\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}.

(6.30)

Similarly, the
matter mid-frequency energy quantity
is defined in the same regime, and is given by

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\upphi,mid}(t)\coloneqq\uppsi_{\lambda}^{2}+\upphi_{\lambda}^{2}.

(6.31)

Finally, define the
total mid-frequency energy quantity
as the sum
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\upeta,low}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\upphi,low}(t)
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
({(\hat{\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\hat{\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})
be a solution to the linearized Einstein–scalar field system (
4.36
)–(
4.50
) in CMCSH gauge. Then there exists some
\mathring{C}_{mid}
, depending only on the background Kasner spacetime, such that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
and
1\leq\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq\mathring{\tau}
, one has

\mathring{C}_{mid}^{-1}\,\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t)\leq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(\tau_{\lambda}^{-1}(\mathring{\tau}))\leq\mathring{C}_{mid}\,\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t).

(6.32)

Enlarging the constant
\mathring{C}_{mid}
if necessary, one has the following equivalence between the high-frequency energy
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(t)
at
t=\tau_{\lambda}^{-1}(\mathring{\tau})
and the low-frequency energy
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t)
at
t=t_{\lambda*}=\tau_{\lambda}^{-1}(1)
.

\mathring{C}_{mid}^{-1}\,\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*})\leq\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(\tau_{\lambda}^{-1}(\mathring{\tau}))\leq\mathring{C}_{mid}\,\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*}).

(6.33)

Proof.

The mid-frequency energy estimate (
6.32
) will be much more straightforward to show than either of the high- or low-frequency energy estimates, since we are working in a bounded domain
\log\tau\in[0,\log\mathring{\tau}]
, and therefore, by (
3.9
), a bounded domain of
\log( \frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}} )\subset[0,C\log\mathring{\tau}]
. As a result, it suffices to show a derivative estimate of the form:

\left|t\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t)\right|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t).

(6.34)

To prove (
6.34
), we use the same
\mathring{g}
-norm that was introduced in Section
5.2
; then by definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}
,

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t)=|\hat{\upeta}_{\lambda}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}+|\hat{\upkappa}_{\lambda}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}+\upphi_{\lambda}^{2}+\uppsi_{\lambda}^{2}.

Further, using the same proof as Proposition
5.5
it is straightforward to show that
|\upnu_{\lambda}|^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t)
and
|\upchi_{\lambda}|_{\mathring{g}}^{2}\lesssim t^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t)
. From this point, (
6.34
) follows immediately upon differentiating
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t)
term-by-term using (
4.36
)–(
4.41
), and applying Cauchy-Schwarz.

Using the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(t_{\lambda*})=\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*})
, while
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(\tau^{-1}(\mathring{\tau}))\asymp\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(\tau^{-1}(\mathring{\tau}))
by Lemma
5.1
, the final estimate (
6.33
) follows immediately from (
6.32
).
∎

7
Scattering for the linearized Einstein–scalar field equations

In Section
7
, we prove our scattering result for the linearized Einstein–scalar field system around Kasner spacetimes satisfying the subcriticality condition, Theorem
1.4
. Recall that the gauge used in Theorem
1.4
is a CMCTC gauge with vanishing shift,
\upchi^{j}=0
, though we do permit changes of gauge associated to a
time-independent
vector field
\upxi^{j}
using (
1.36
)–(
1.38
).

On the other hand, our energy estimates use various different gauges; though the low-frequency energy estimate of Section
6
used a CMCTC gauge, our high-frequency energy estimate of Section
5
crucially used a CMCSH gauge (as did the mid-frequency estimate of Section
6.4
, though this was not as crucial).

As a result, we instead first prove a version of Theorem
1.4
not in the CMCTC gauge, but in a gauge called the
Constant Mean Curvature Frequency Adapted
(CMCFA) gauge which we introduce in Section
7.1
. This reinterpreted scattering result, Theorem
7.1
, is stated in Section
7.2
and its proof will be in Section
7.3
.

In Section
7.4
, we understand how to translate back to CMCTC gauge, and thereby complete the proofs of the remaining theorems regarding the linearized Einstein–scalar field system, that is the scattering result Theorem
1.4
and also the asymptotics of Theorem
1.2
.

7.1
Frequency adapted norms and gauges

As well as the gauge issue mentioned above, we make the following comments about Theorem
1.4
; the idea being that these comments will inform how our second version, Theorem
7.1
, will differ:

(I)

The variables
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi)
used to describe Cauchy data at
t=1
differ from the variables
({\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi,\upvarphi)
used to describe the asymptotic data at
t=0
.

(II)

In retrieving the optimal regularity of (
1.43
), we require a gauge transformation associated to a vector field
\upxi^{j}
. (Furthermore, the regularity of
\upxi^{j}
cannot be controlled particularly well.)

(III)

The regularity statements (
1.43
)–(
1.44
), and the spaces
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty}
, feature a correction by a term involving the symbol
\mathcal{T}_{*}
; in particular
\mathcal{T}_{*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}
often acts on tensorial quantities such as
{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
.

These issues are inevitable, in light of the energy estimates presented in Section
5
and Section
6
. This is since both the relevant gauge and the metrics
\mathring{g}_{ij}(t)
and
\mathring{g}_{ij}(t_{\lambda*})
used to define the energies differ between the high-frequency and the low-frequency regimes.

Thus, in Theorem
7.1
, we first present a version of Theorem
1.4
that will follow more cleanly from our energy estimates. In preparation, we make a series of definitions corresponding to (I)–(III) above. Each of theses definitions will involve the symbol
\mathcal{T}_{*}
, see Definition
1.1
. We start by defining new renormalized quantities in order to deal with (I).

Definition 7.1
.

We define the following
frequency adapted renormalized quantity

{}^{(F)}{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
:

{}^{(F)}{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\coloneqq{\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+\int^{\mathcal{T}_{*}}_{\min\{t,\mathcal{T}_{*}\}}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\cdot{\upkappa}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}={\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}-\int^{1}_{\max\{t,\mathcal{T}_{*}\}}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\cdot{\upkappa}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}},

(7.1)

as well as the following
frequency adapted scalar field quantity

{}^{(F)}\upvarphi
:

{}^{(F)}\upvarphi\coloneqq\upphi+\max\left\{0,\log\left(\frac{\mathcal{T}_{*}}{t}\right)\right\}\cdot\uppsi=\upvarphi+\max\{\log( \mathcal{T}_* ),\log t\}\cdot\uppsi.

(7.2)

Remark
.

The point is that the Fourier coefficients
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
coincide with
{(\upeta_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
in the high-frequency regime
t\geq t_{\lambda*}
, while
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
coincides with
{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
in the low-frequency regime
t\leq t_{\lambda*}
; here
{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
is defined in (
7.1
). Similarly,
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda}
coincides with
\upphi_{\lambda}
at high frequency and with
\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}
at low frequency.

Next, we define a new (linearized) gauge condition used to deal with (II).

Definition 7.2
.

We say that a solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
solving the linearized Einstein–scalar field system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) is in a linearized
Constant Mean Curvature Frequency Adapted
(CMCFA) gauge if frequency adapted renormalized quantity
{}^{(F)}{{\Upupsilon}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
satisfies the differential relation

\partial_{i}\tr\,\!{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon-2\partial_{j}{}^{(F)}{{\Upupsilon}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}=0.

(7.3)

Finally, we define a norm for
(1,1)
tensor fields to deal with (III). We shall often apply this to the
(1,1)
tensor fields
{}^{(F)}{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and also the derivative of the shift,
\partial_{i}\upchi^{j}
, viewed as a
(1,1)
tensor field.

Definition 7.3
.

Let
A={A}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
be a
(1,1)
tensor field on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
. Define the
frequency adapted
L^{2}
norm
of
A
, thought to live on the boundary
\{t=0\}
of
\mathcal{M}_{Kas}
, via the following:

\|{A}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{L^{2,F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}\coloneqq\sum_{i,j=1}^{D}\|\mathcal{T}_{*}^{-p_{i}+p_{j}}{A}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\mathring{g}^{ac}(\mathcal{T}_{*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(\mathcal{T}_{*})\cdot{A}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{A}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}.

Equivalently, one has the frequency space definition:

\|{A}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{L^{2,F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}}\mathring{g}^{ac}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(t_{\lambda*}){(A_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{(A_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}.

One further defines the
frequency adapted
H^{s}
norm
of
A
using:

\|{A}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s}\mathring{g}^{ac}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(t_{\lambda*}){(A_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{(A_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}.

(7.4)

7.2
Statement of scattering for the linearized Einstein–scalar field system, v2

Theorem 7.1

(Scattering for the linearized Einstein–scalar field system in subcritical Kasner spacetimes, v2)
.

Let
(\mathcal{M}_{Kas},g_{Kas},\phi_{Kas})
be a (generalized) Kasner spacetime whose exponents satisfy the subcriticality condition (
2.22
) with
\updelta>0
. Consider solutions
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
to the linearized Einstein–scalar field system of Proposition
4.2
around
(\mathcal{M}_{Kas},g_{Kas},\phi_{Kas})
, with the solution such that the CMCFA gauge condition (
7.3
) holds. Then the scattering theory is as follows:

(i)

(Existence of the scattering operator)
Let
s
be sufficiently large. Then given Cauchy data
({(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{C},\uppsi_{C})\in H^{s+1}\times H^{s}\times H^{s+1}\times H^{s}
at
t=1
satisfying the constraint equations (
4.15
)–(
4.20
) and gauge conditions (
7.3
) and
\tr\upkappa_{C}=0
, there exists a unique (classical) solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi)
to the system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) satisfying
\tr\upkappa=0
and the CMCFA condition (
7.3
), which attains the Cauchy data:

{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot)={\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot)={(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(\cdot),\quad{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot)={(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(\cdot),\quad{}^{(F)}{\upvarphi}(1,\cdot)=\upphi(1,\cdot)=\upphi_{C}(\cdot),\quad\uppsi(1,\cdot)=\uppsi_{C}(\cdot).

Furthermore, for some
\beta>0
depending only on the background
(\mathcal{M}_{Kas},g_{Kas})
, there exist unique
(1,1)
tensors
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
, and functions
\uppsi_{\infty}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}
on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
, such that

\displaystyle{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t,\cdot)\to{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(\cdot),\quad{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t,\cdot)\to{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(\cdot)\quad\text{ in }H^{s-\beta}\text{ strongly as }t\to 0,

(7.5)

\displaystyle\uppsi(t,\cdot)\to\uppsi_{\infty}(\cdot),\quad{}^{(F)}\upvarphi(t,\cdot)\to{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}(\cdot)\quad\text{ in }H^{s}\text{ strongly as }t\to 0.

(7.6)

These limiting objects
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
\uppsi_{\infty}
and
\upphi_{\infty}
obey the asymptotic constraints (
4.25
)–(
4.29
) (with
{\tilde{\Upupsilon}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
\tilde{\upvarphi}
replaced by
{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi
respectively, and
T
replaced by the symbol
\mathcal{T}_{*}
) as well as the limiting frequency adapted gauge condition:

\partial_{i}\tr\,\!{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty}-2\partial_{j}{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}=0.

(7.7)

Finally, for the frequency adapted Sobolev spaces
H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})
in Definition
7.3
, one has

{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0}),\qquad\uppsi_{\infty},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.8)

(ii)

(Asymptotic completeness)
Let
s
be sufficiently large, and let
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
be
(1,1)
tensor fields and
\uppsi_{\infty}
,
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}
be functions on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
verifying the constraints (
4.25
)–(
4.29
) (modified as in (i)), with regularity as in (
7.8
), and obeying the gauge condition (
7.7
). Then there exists a unique (classical) solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
to the system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) satisfying
\tr\upkappa=0
and the CMCFA condition (
7.3
), such that as
t\to 0
, the asymptotics (
7.5
)–(
7.6
) hold.

The regularity of the solution will be as follows:

\displaystyle({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}})\in C^{0}((0,+\infty),H^{s+1}\times H^{s})\cap C^{1}((0,+\infty),H^{s}\times H^{s-1}),

\displaystyle(\upphi,\uppsi)\in C^{0}((0,+\infty),H^{s+1}\times H^{s})\cap C^{1}((0,+\infty),H^{s}\times H^{s-1}),

\displaystyle(\upnu,\upchi^{j})\in C^{0}((0,+\infty),H^{s+2}\times H^{s+2}).

(iii)

(Scattering isomorphism)
For
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the following Hilbert spaces together with their norms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{s}_{C}=\left\{\left({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi\right):{\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\in H^{s+1},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\in H^{s},\upphi\in H^{s+1},\uppsi\in H^{s}\right\},

(7.9)

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{C}}^{2}\coloneqq\|{\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\upphi\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|\uppsi\|_{H^{s}}^{2},

(7.10)

\displaystyle{}^{(F)}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty}=\left\{\left({\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi,\upvarphi\right):{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0}),{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0}),\uppsi\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}},\upvarphi\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\right\},

(7.11)

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi,\upvarphi\right)\right\|_{{}^{(F)}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty}}^{2}=\|{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}+\|{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}+\|\uppsi\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|\upvarphi\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

(7.12)

Further, let
\mathcal{H}_{C,c}^{s}
be the subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{C}
obeying the constraint equations (
4.15
)–(
4.20
) as well as the gauge conditions
\tr\upkappa=0
and (
7.3
), and
{}^{(F)}\mathcal{H}_{\infty,c}^{s}
be the subspace of
{}^{(F)}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty}
obeying the asymptotic constraints (
4.51
)–(
4.55
), modified as in (i), and the gauge conditions
\tr\upkappa=0
and (
7.7
).

Then the map
{}^{(F)}\mathcal{S}_{\downarrow}
, which is defined to map
({(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{C},\uppsi_{C})
from (
i
) to the limiting quantities
({(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\infty},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty})
, may be extended to a Hilbert space isomorphism
{}^{(F)}\mathcal{S}_{\downarrow}:\mathcal{H}_{C,c}^{s}\to{}^{(F)}\mathcal{H}_{\infty,c}^{s}
, whose inverse
{}^{(F)}\mathcal{S}_{\uparrow}={}^{(F)}{\mathcal{S}}_{\downarrow}^{-1}
is exactly given by (a suitable extension of) the map taking
({(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}{\Upupsilon}_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\infty},{}^{(F)}{\upvarphi}_{\infty})
in (
ii
) to
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot),{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot),\upphi(1,\cdot),\uppsi(1,\cdot))
.

7.3
Proof of scattering for the linearized Einstein–scalar field system, v2

As in Section
3.4
for the case of the wave equation, we first assume smoothness for all our variables and derive a priori estimates between Sobolev spaces, then use the density of smooth functions to recover the full scattering theorem. As in Section
3.4
, we have two propositions, providing the a priori estimates in both directions of the scattering theory. Before proceeding, we determine what happens at zero frequency.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
be a solution of the linearized Einstein–scalar field system of Proposition
4.2
, in any gauge. Then with
{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
\upvarphi
as in Definition
1.2
, the
0
-frequency Fourier coefficients

({(\upkappa_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\Upupsilon_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{0},\upvarphi_{0})
are constant, in particular for all
t>0
:

{(\upkappa_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)={(\upkappa_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(0),\quad{(\Upupsilon_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)={(\Upupsilon_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(0),\quad\uppsi_{0}(t)=\uppsi_{0}(1),\qquad\upvarphi_{0}(t)=\upvarphi_{0}(1).

(7.13)

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition
4.7
in the case that
\lambda=0
.
∎

7.3.1
From Cauchy data to asymptotic data

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
{(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
be smooth
(1,1)
tensors on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
, and
\upphi_{C}
and
\uppsi_{C}
be smooth functions on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
, obeying the constraint equations (
4.15
)–(
4.20
) with
t=1
, as well as (
7.3
) and
\tr\upkappa_{C}=0
. Then there exists a unique
16
16

16

For uniqueness, one requires as always that
\upchi^{j}
has zero average.
smooth solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
to the system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) for
t>0
, satisfying
\tr\upkappa=0
and the CMCFA gauge condition (
7.3
), that achieves the initial data.

Moreover, for some
\beta>0
, there exist smooth
(1,1)
-tensors
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
, and smooth functions
\uppsi_{\infty}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}
, such that for
s\in\mathbb{R}
and the solution as above, the following strong convergence holds as
t\to 0
, where the frequency adapted quantities
{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi
are given in Definition
7.1
:

{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)\to{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\quad{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)\to{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\text{ in }H^{s-\beta},\quad\uppsi(t)\to\uppsi_{\infty},\quad{}^{(F)}\upvarphi(t)\to{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}\text{ in }H^{s}.

(7.14)

For the
H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})
norms of Definition
7.3
, one has

\|{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}+\|{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}+\|\upphi_{\infty}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\\[3.99994pt]
\lesssim\|{(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\upphi_{C}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|\uppsi_{C}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

(7.15)

Finally, the quantities
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
,
\uppsi_{\infty}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}
satisfy the constraints (
4.25
)–(
4.29
), with
T=\mathcal{T}_{*}
.

Proof.

We start with existence and uniqueness of a smooth solution for
t>0
. For this purpose, we firstly apply Proposition
4.4
(ii) with
\upchi^{j}_{\circ}=0
, which gives us a unique smooth solution
({\tilde{\upeta}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\tilde{\upkappa}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu)
in the CMCTC gauge (i.e. with vanishing
\upchi^{j}
).

However, we would like existence and uniqueness in the CMCFA gauge instead. In light of Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.6
, this may be achieved via the change of gauge associated to the vector field
\upxi^{j}
, with

\mathcal{T}_{M}^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}(\mathcal{T}_{M})\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\upxi^{j}=\mathcal{T}_{M}^{2}\mathring{g}^{jk}(\mathcal{T}_{M})(\partial_{k}\tr\tilde{\Upupsilon}-2\partial_{\ell}{\tilde{\Upupsilon}}_{{k}}^{\phantom{{k}}{\ell}}),\quad\text{ where }\mathcal{T}_{M}=\max\{t,\mathcal{T}_{*}\}.

(7.16)

The symbol
\mathcal{T}_{M}=\max\{t,\mathcal{T}_{*}\}
arises due to the definition of
{}^{(F)}{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
. Using this change of gauge, one has existence in the CMCFA gauge. Uniqueness also follows by a similar argument to uniqueness in Proposition
4.4
(b) – essentially reverse this change of gauge and use uniqueness in CMCTC.

Given our solution, we now take the Fourier decomposition. From this point, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
, we shall always work in the variables
({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})
, and that by definition of the CMCFA gauge, we have the following description in the high-frequency regime
t_{\lambda*}\geq 1
and the low-frequency regime
0<t_{\lambda*}\leq 1
: for the high-frequency (and mid-frequency) regime
t_{\lambda*}\geq 1
,

({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})=({(\hat{\upkappa}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\hat{\upeta}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upphi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})

and the
\lambda
-Fourier projections in this range are in the CMCSH gauge, i.e. they satisfy (
4.35
). This means that we can apply our high-frequency energy estimates of Section
5
.

On the other hand, in the low-frequency regime
0<t_{\lambda*}\leq 1
, we have

({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})=({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}),

with
{(\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda}
as in (
6.1
) and (
6.2
). Here, we do not quite have
\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}=0
as in the CMCTC gauge used in Section
6
, and instead satisfy the CMCFA gauge condition
2\lambda_{j}{}^{(F)}{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}=\lambda_{i}\tr\,\!{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon
. Nevertheless, as we see shortly it is not a major difficulty to go back and forth from CMCFA gauge to CMCTC gauge in the low-frequency regime, and one may still apply the low-frequency energy estimates of Section
6
.

We first relate our energies to the initial data
({(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{C},\upphi_{C})
. Splitting into high-frequencies and mid-frequences depending on
\tau_{\lambda}(1)
, and using Lemma
5.1
in the high-frequency case, we relate the energies to Sobolev norms of initial data:

|{(\upkappa_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}+|{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}+\uppsi_{0}^{2}+{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{0}^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}\\
\tau_{\lambda}(1)<\mathring{\tau}\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+1}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}\\
\tau_{\lambda}(1)\geq\mathring{\tau}\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+1}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(1)\\[3.00003pt]
\asymp\|{(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\upphi_{C}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|\uppsi_{C}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

(7.17)

Applying the high-frequency energy estimate Proposition
5.2
, as well as the mid-frequency energy estimate Proposition
5.2
(which both use the CMCSH as in our case for
t\geq t_{\lambda*}
), we deduce that

|{(\upkappa_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}+|{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}+\uppsi_{0}^{2}+{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{0}^{2}+\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+1}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}(t_{\lambda*})\\[3.99994pt]
\lesssim\|{(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\upphi_{C}\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|\uppsi_{C}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

(7.18)

We next apply the low-frequency energy estimate Proposition
6.2
. However, since this proposition assumes the CMCTC gauge, we need to find a way to translate the energy estimate. Our method is as follows: we first generate another solution
({({}^{(T)}\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(T)}\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},\tilde{\upvarphi}_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda})
which is in the CMCTC gauge and which
agrees with our solution
at
t=t_{\lambda*}
, and thus to which Proposition
6.2
and Proposition
6.4
apply, then transform into CMCFA gauge using some change of gauge vector field
\upxi_{\lambda}^{j}
.

This change of gauge vector field agrees exactly with (
7.16
) above. Furthermore, projecting (
7.16
) onto low frequencies with
0<t\leq t_{\lambda*}
where by definition,
\tau^{2}(t_{\lambda*})=t_{\lambda*}^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}(t_{\lambda*})\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}=1
,
\upxi_{\lambda}^{j}
is exactly given by

\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}=t_{\lambda*}^{2}\mathring{g}^{jk}(t_{\lambda*})[\lambda_{k}\tr\,\!{}^{(T)}\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda}-2\lambda_{\ell}{({}^{(T)}\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{k}}^{\phantom{{k}}{\ell}}].

(7.19)

Upon checking the
t_{\lambda*}
weights carefully, Propositions
6.2
and
6.4
tell us that

|t_{\lambda*}^{p_{j}-1}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}(t_{\lambda*}),\qquad\left|t\frac{t}{dt}(t_{\lambda*}^{p_{j}-1}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda})\right|\lesssim\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,low}^{1/2}(t_{\lambda*}).

(7.20)

Next, the change of gauge vector field
\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}
relates the two gauges by (see (
4.22
), (
4.33
) and (
4.23
)):

\displaystyle{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}={({}^{(T)}\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}-\mathrm{i}(t{\mathring{k}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{p}})\lambda_{p}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}+\mathrm{i}(t{\mathring{k}}_{{p}}^{\phantom{{p}}{j}})\lambda_{i}\upxi^{p}_{\lambda},

\displaystyle{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}={({}^{(T)}\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\lambda_{i}\upxi_{\lambda}^{j}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\mathring{g}_{ip}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}^{jq}(t_{\lambda*})\lambda_{q}\upxi_{\lambda}^{p},

\displaystyle\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}=-t\frac{t}{dt}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}.

By (
7.20
),
\lambda_{i}\upxi_{\lambda}^{j}
satisfies the same estimates as either of
{({}^{(T)}\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
or
{({}^{(T)}\tilde{\Upupsilon}_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
. So all the low-frequency energy estimates of Section
6
apply equally well to
({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{((F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}})
in our CMCFA gauge.

In particular, from the second estimate in (
7.20
) we deduce an analogue of Proposition
6.5
(i), in other words we have unique tensors
{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{(({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
, and real numbers
(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}
and
({}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty})_{\lambda}
with the CMCFA analogue of the estimates (
6.28
)–(
6.29
). We now simply define

{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(x)={(\upkappa_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}}{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\,e^{i\lambda\cdot x},\qquad{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(x)={({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{0})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}}{(({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\,e^{i\lambda\cdot x},

\uppsi_{\infty}(x)=\uppsi_{0}+\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}}(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}\,e^{i\lambda\cdot x},\qquad{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}(x)={}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{0}+\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}}({}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty})_{\lambda}\,e^{i\lambda\cdot x}.

The final estimate in Proposition
6.5
(i), combined with (
7.18
) and the definition of
H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})
then yields (
7.15
). Since this is true for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})\subset H^{s-\beta}
for some
\beta>0
independent of
s
, this tells us that our limiting tensors and functions are smooth.

It remains to show the convergence (
7.14
). Let us first take some
r\in\mathbb{R}
, and estimate

\|{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}-{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{r}}^{2}=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r}|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}-{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}.

We estimate this by splitting into low and high frequencies, and then applying the energy estimates. At low frequencies, we shall use the convergence (
6.28
), while at high and mid frequencies we only use boundedness. For convenience, let us define:

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(1)&\text{ if }\tau_{\lambda}(1)\geq\mathring{\tau},\\
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,mid}(1)&\text{ if }1\leq\tau_{\lambda}(1)<\mathring{\tau}.\end{cases}

(7.21)

Using the energy estimates, one then finds:

\displaystyle\|{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)-{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D},\\
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r}|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)-{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D},\\
\tau_{\lambda}(t)>1\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r}(|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2}+|{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}|^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D},\\
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r}t_{\lambda*}^{2p_{i}-2p_{j}}\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{2\updelta}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\\
\tau_{\lambda}(t)>1\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r}(\tau t^{2p_{i}-2p_{j}}+t_{\lambda*}^{2p_{i}-2p_{j}})\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1).

To finally show convergence, we select an
N>0
such that the following quantities are bounded, uniformly in
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
:

\langle\lambda\rangle^{-N}t_{\lambda*}^{2p_{i}-2p_{j}-2\updelta}+\langle\lambda\rangle^{-N}\tau\max_{t\geq t_{\lambda*}}\{t^{2p_{i}-2p_{j}}\}\lesssim 1.

This is always possible, since
\tau\leq\langle\lambda\rangle
, while
t_{\lambda*}^{-1}
grows polynomially in
\langle\lambda\rangle
. Substituting this into the above, we therefore find that

\|{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}-{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{r}}^{2}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D},\\
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1\end{subarray}}t^{2\updelta}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r-N}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D},\\
\tau_{\lambda}(t)>1\end{subarray}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2r-N}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1).

Selecting
2r-N=2s+1
, we therefore deduce the
{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
convergence in (
7.14
) for
\beta=\frac{1+N}{2}
. The
{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
convergence follows similarly (the additional difficulty being that we need to incorporate the bound for
G_{ip}^{\phantom{ip}jq}
in Lemma
6.3
, and thus may have to increase
\beta
further), while the
\uppsi
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi
convergence follow from similar considerations to Proposition
3.8
.

Since
H^{s-\beta}\subset C^{3}
for
s
sufficiently large, the fact that our limiting quantities satisfy the relevant constraints follows from a version of Lemma
4.5
with
T=\mathcal{T}_{*}
. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

7.3.2
From asymptotic data to Cauchy data

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
be smooth
(1,1)
tensors and
\uppsi_{\infty},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}
be smooth functions on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
satisfying the asymptotic constraints (
4.25
)–(
4.29
) with
T=\mathcal{T}_{*}
. Then there exists a unique smooth solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
to the system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) for
t>0
in CMCFA gauge, which achieves the asymptotic data in the sense that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\beta
as in Propostion
7.3
, one has the (strong) convergence:

{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)\to{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\quad{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)\to{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\text{ in }H^{s-\beta},\quad\uppsi(t)\to\uppsi_{\infty},\quad{}^{(F)}\upvarphi(t)\to{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}\text{ in }H^{s}.

(7.22)

Moreover, for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
one has the following bound between Sobolev spaces:

\|{\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot)\|_{H^{s+1}}+\|{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1,\cdot)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\upphi(1,\cdot)\|_{H^{s+1}}^{2}+\|\uppsi(1,\cdot)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\\[3.99994pt]
\lesssim\|{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}+\|{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2},F}(\Sigma_{0})}^{2}+\|\upphi_{\infty}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

(7.23)

Proof.

For existence, we decompose the scattering data
({(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\infty},{}^{(F)}\upphi_{\infty})
into their Fourier modes. For any
\lambda\neq 0
, we now appeal to Proposition
6.5
(ii), which tells us that there exists a solution to the ODE system (
6.3
)–(
6.6
) in the range
t\in(0,t_{\lambda*})
, which achieves the relevant limits as
t\to 0
, at the rate (
6.28
)–(
6.29
).

However, the ODE system (
6.3
)–(
6.6
) is in the CMCTC gauge, as opposed to the CMCFA gauge as required. Nevertheless, as in the proof of Proposition
7.3
, we may use a change of gauge associated to the vector field
\upxi_{\lambda}^{j}
in (
7.19
) to generate another solution in the CMCFA gauge. As in that proof, we still have the estimates (
7.20
), and as a result the energy estimates of Section
6
will all remain valid in this gauge. We denote this solution in CMCFA gauge to be
({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})
as before.

We extend this solution to the range
t>t_{\lambda*}
by using instead the CMCSH gauge evolution; this is valid since the CMCSH gauge and the CMCFA gauge coincide for
t\geq t_{\lambda*}
. Then combining all of the low-frequency estimates in Proposition
6.5
, the mid-frequency estimate in Proposition
6.6
and the high-frequency estimate of Proposition
5.2
, we find that for
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)
as in (
7.21
), our solution obeys:

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)\lesssim\mathring{g}^{ac}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(t_{\lambda*})\left[{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}+{(({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{(({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}\right]+(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}+({}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}.

(7.24)

Using Lemma
7.2
to deal with
\lambda=0
, and using the coercivity of the high-frequency energy in Lemma
5.1
, we therefore have the estimate

\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s}|{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1)|^{2}+\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+2}|{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(1)|^{2}+\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s}|\uppsi_{\lambda}(1)|^{2}+\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+2}|{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda}(1)|^{2}\\[3.99994pt]
\lesssim\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+1}\left[\mathring{g}^{ac}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}_{bd}(t_{\lambda*})\left[{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{((\upkappa_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}+{(({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{a}}^{\phantom{{a}}{b}}{(({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})_{\lambda})}_{{c}}^{\phantom{{c}}{d}}\right]+(\uppsi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}+({}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{2}\right].

By smoothness of the scattering data, the right-hand side is summable in
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}
for all
s
, and doing this sum yields the estimate (
7.23
).

Now, upon applying Fourier inversion we recover a smooth solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
to the system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) for
t>0
in CMCFA gauge, which moreover achieves the asymptotics (
7.22
) using the same proof as in Proposition
7.3
. We leave the details to the reader.

All that remains is to show uniqueness. Since the system is linear it suffices to show uniqueness in the case of trivial scattering data
{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}=0
,
{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}=0
,
\uppsi_{\infty}=0
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty}=0
. Suppose that we have a smooth solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upxi^{j})
to (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) in the CMCFA gauge, achieving these trivial asymptotics in the sense of (
7.22
). To show that this solution vanishes, it will suffice to show each Fourier mode vanishes.

The
0
-Fourier mode must vanish by Lemma
7.2
, so now take any
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
, and focus on the low-frequency regime with
0<t\leq t_{\lambda*}
. Recall that Proposition
6.5
(ii) gives us uniqueness in CMCTC gauge, and for uniqueness in CMCFA gauge we look for a gauge transformation that retains the trivial asymptotic data but so that the transformed solution is in CMCTC gauge.

The idea is that any
\lambda
-mode projected solution
({(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\lambda},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})
in CMCFA gauge must obey
|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)|\leq Dt^{\updelta}
for some constant
D
that may depend on data but does not depend on
t
. This follows from (
7.19
) and the description of
\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}
in the proof of Proposition
7.3
. (In other words, that any CMCFA solution may be constructed from another CMCTC solution, though this latter CMCTC solution will not necessarily have trivial asymptotic data.)

We find a CMCTC solution with trivial asymptotic data via the gauge transformation associated to the vector field
{}^{(T)}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)=\int^{t}_{0}\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}\frac{ds}{s}
. Since
|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)|\leq Dt^{\updelta}
,
{}^{(T)}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)
is well-defined and vanishes at
t=0
. Further, by Lemma
4.3
(ii) with
T=0
, it transforms our CMCFA solution to a CMCTC solution which still has trivial asymptotic data. Thus by uniqueness of CMCTC solutions from asymptotic data, the transformed solution must vanish. By (
4.33
) with
T=t_{\lambda*}
, this tells us that

{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\lambda_{i}{}^{(T)}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\mathring{g}_{ip}(t_{\lambda*})\mathring{g}^{jq}(t_{\lambda*})\lambda_{q}{}^{(T)}\upxi^{q}_{\lambda}.

We now simply insert this into our frequency adapted gauge condition (
7.3
). Eventually we obtain
\mathring{g}^{ab}(t_{\lambda*})\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}{}^{(T)}\upxi_{\lambda}^{j}=0
. In other words, the gauge transformation vector field has
{}^{(T)}\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}=0
, and our solution must have been trivial in the first place. This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

7.3.3
Completion of the proof of Theorem
7.1

All the remains is to extend Proposition
7.3
and Proposition
7.4
to the setting where
({\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi,{}^{(F)}\upvarphi)
have finite regularity, by using the density of smooth functions in
H^{s}
and in
H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})
. This is a slightly more complicated version of what was done for the wave equation in Section
3.4.3
, and therefore we leave the details to the reader, with only 2 comments:

–

In both directions, the data
({(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{C},\uppsi_{C})
or
({(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{({}^{(F)}\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\uppsi_{\infty},{}^{(F)}\upvarphi_{\infty})
is not completely free, and must be arranged to satisfy various constraint equations and gauge conditions. Thus in approximating finite regularity data by smooth data, the approximating data must also satisfy these equations.

However, since in our case the constraint equations and gauge conditions are linear, it is easy to smoothly approximate data while still satisfying these equations, for instance by convolution with standard mollifiers.

–

In order to insist both that our solutions are classical and to make sure that the convergence (
7.5
) is also in
C^{3}
in order to satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
4.5
, we must choose
s>\frac{D}{2}+3+\beta
. This is what we mean by requiring
s
to be sufficiently large in (i) and (ii). For (iii), the Hilbert space isomorphism remains true for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

This concludes our proof of Theorem
7.1
. ∎

7.4
Proof of scattering in transported coordinates

In this section, we prove the original version of the scattering result, Theorem
1.4
, in a CMCTC gauge, and finally derive the asymptotics of Theorem
1.2
concening the linearized metric and second fundamental form. To go from CMCFA gauge to CMCTC gauge, we use Lemma
4.3
(ii); thus it remains to understand
\upchi^{j}
in CMCFA gauge, or more precisely the related change of gauge vector field

\upxi^{j}(t)\coloneqq-\int^{1}_{t}\upchi^{j}(s)\,\frac{ds}{s}.

(7.25)

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
be a solution to the linearized Einstein–scalar field system (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) in CMCFA gauge, with regularity as in either of (i) or (ii) of Theorem
7.1
. Then the change of gauge vector field
\upxi^{j}
defined in (
7.25
) satisfies

\upxi^{j}\in C^{0}((0,+\infty):H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}).

Moreover there exists a unique vector field
\upxi^{j}_{\infty}\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}
such that
\upxi^{j}(t)\to\upxi_{\infty}^{j}
in
H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}
as
t\to 0
.

Proof.

Let us first work at the level of each Fourier mode
({(\upeta_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\upkappa_{\lambda})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{\lambda},\uppsi_{\lambda},\upnu_{\lambda},\upchi^{j}_{\lambda})
, where
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}
(recall that
\upchi^{j}
and thus
\upxi^{j}
have zero average). Using (
5.8
) from Lemma
5.5
, and unpacking the
\mathring{g}
-norm, we find that for
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\geq\mathring{\tau}
,

|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)|\lesssim\frac{t^{1-p_{j}}}{\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}^{1/2}(1).

(7.26)

Here we have used the high-frequency energy estimate Proposition
5.2
to allow the right-hand side to be
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)
, as defined in (
7.21
), in place of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}(t)
. Using also the mid-frequency energy estimate Proposition
6.6
and the associated elliptic estimates, (
7.26
) also holds in the mid-frequency range, and thus for the whole range
\tau_{\lambda}(t)\geq 1
.

In the low-frequency regime, we estimate
|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}|
by referring to the proof of Proposition
7.3
, in particular (
7.20
) and the description of
\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}
immediately below it. Combining all the energy estimates, one deduces that in the low-frequency range
0<\tau_{\lambda}(t)\leq 1
, one has

|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)|\lesssim t_{\lambda*}^{1-p_{j}}\cdot\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}^{1/2}(1).

(7.27)

Upon using that
t\leq 1
and
t_{\lambda*}\leq 1
in our setting, we combine (
7.26
) and (
7.27
) to get that for all
t>0
,

|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)|\lesssim\min\left\{\tau^{-\frac{3}{2}},\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta}\right\}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}^{1/2}(1).

(7.28)

(
7.28
) implies that
|\upchi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)|
is integrable (with respect to the differential
\frac{dt}{t}
) both towards
\tau\to\infty
and towards
t\to 0
. Indeed, using also Lemma
3.2
, it is straightforward to see that
\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)
, defined in (
7.25
), has a limit
(\upxi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{j}
as
t\to 0
, and we have

|(\upxi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{j}|\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{1/2}_{\lambda,high}(1),\qquad|\upxi^{j}_{\lambda}(t)-(\upxi_{\infty})_{\lambda}^{j}|\lesssim\max\left\{\left(\frac{t}{t_{\lambda*}}\right)^{\updelta},1\right\}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,high}^{1/2}(1).

(7.29)

To complete the proof, we recall that in Theorem
7.1
(i) and (ii), we have
\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}\setminus\{0\}}\langle\lambda\rangle^{2s+1}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,hm}(1)<+\infty
. The lemma then follows straightforwardly from (
7.29
).
∎

With Lemma
7.5
, we now deduce Theorem
1.4
and Theorem
1.2
from Theorem
7.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

We prove (
i
), (
ii
) and (
iii
) as follows:

(i)

Let
({(\upeta_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{(\upkappa_{C})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi_{C},\uppsi_{C})
be Cauchy data with regularity as given and satisfying the constraints (
4.15
)–(
4.20
). We now apply Theorem
7.1
, which gives us a classical solution
({\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}},\upphi,\uppsi,\upnu,\upchi^{j})
of (
4.6
)–(
4.20
) in the CMCFA gauge. There are two steps to get to the setup of Theorem
1.4
.

Firstly, even before changing gauge, we upgrade the convergence of the frequency adapted quantities
{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi
to convergence of
{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
\upvarphi
in Definition
1.2
. From Definition
7.1
, we have

{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}={{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+\int^{1}_{\max\{t,\mathcal{T}_{*}\}}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\cdot{\upkappa}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}},\quad\upvarphi={}^{(F)}\upvarphi-\max\{\log( \mathcal{T}_* ),\log t\}\cdot\uppsi.

(7.30)

Here, the object involving integral in (
7.30
) is a time-dependent symbol which, in some symbol class
S^{\beta_{0}}
for
\beta>0
, converges (strongly) as follows:

\int^{1}_{\max\{t,\mathcal{T}_{*}\}}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)\,\frac{ds}{s}\to\int^{1}_{\mathcal{T}_{*}}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{jq}(s)\,\frac{ds}{s}

Therefore, for
s
sufficiently large, the convergence (
7.5
) together with Sobolev embedding implies the existence of
{(\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
such that the convergence (
1.41
) also holds, at least in CMCFA gauge. A similar result holds for
\upvarphi
, and here we can get convergence in
H^{s}
by the same proof as Proposition
3.8
.

To transform back to CMCTC gauge, we use the change of gauge associated to the vector field
\upxi^{j}
defined in (
7.25
). By (
4.22
) and (
4.33
),
{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
transform as:

{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\mapsto{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+(t{\mathring{k}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{p}})\partial_{p}\upxi^{j}-(t{\mathring{k}}_{{p}}^{\phantom{{p}}{j}})\partial_{i}\upxi^{p},\quad{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}\mapsto{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{i}\upxi^{j}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{g}_{ip}(1)\mathring{g}^{jq}(1)\partial_{q}\upxi^{p}.

Since, by Lemma
7.5
, the vector field
\upxi^{j}
also converges to
\upxi_{\infty}^{j}
in
H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}
as
t\to 0
, Sobolev embedding implies that we also have the desired convergence in CMCTC gauge.

Finally, the regularity statement (
1.43
) follows from (
7.8
), upon using (
7.30
) and unraveling the
H^{s,F}(\Sigma_{0})
norm from Definition
7.3
. The remainder of Theorem
1.4
(i) is straightforward.

(ii)

The proof of Theorem
1.4
(ii) follows from an identical manner to the above; we first apply Theorem
7.1
(ii) to find the solution in CMCFA gauge and then transform back using
\upxi^{j}
from (
7.25
).

(iii)

For the scattering isomorphism in Theorem
1.4
(iii), one simply notes that the spaces
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{C,c}
in Theorem
1.4
and Theorem
7.1
are identical, while the difference between
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty,c}
and
{}^{(F)}\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty,c}
exactly encodes going from the frequency adapted quantities
{{}^{(F)}\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{}^{(F)}\upvarphi
back to
{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
to
\upvarphi
. Thus Theorem
1.4
(iii) follows immediately from Theorem
7.1
(iii). ∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The asymptotics of Theorem
1.2
follow straightforwardly from Theorem
1.4
(i) and the definition of the linearly small quantities in Definition
4.1
. The scalar field asymptotics (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) may be read off immediately, while the metric perturbations
h_{ij}=\bar{g}_{ij}-\mathring{\bar{g}}_{ij}
and
\kappa_{ij}=t(k_{ij}-\mathring{k}_{ij})
are related to
{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
(to linear order) as follows:

\displaystyle h_{ij}=-2\mathring{g}_{jk}{\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{k}}=-2\mathring{g}_{jk}{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{k}}+2\mathring{g}_{jk}\int^{1}_{t}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{kq}(s)\frac{ds}{s}\cdot{\upkappa}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}},

\displaystyle k_{ij}=\mathring{g}_{jk}{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{k}}-2\mathring{g}_{jk}(t{\mathring{k}}_{{\ell}}^{\phantom{{\ell}}{k}}){\upeta}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{\ell}}=\mathring{g}_{jk}({\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{k}}-(2t{\mathring{k}}_{{\ell}}^{\phantom{{\ell}}{k}}){\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{\ell}})+\mathring{g}_{jk}(2t{\mathring{k}}_{{\ell}}^{\phantom{{\ell}}{k}})\int^{1}_{t}\mathring{g}_{ip}(s)\mathring{g}^{\ell q}(s)\frac{ds}{s}\cdot{\upkappa}_{{q}}^{\phantom{{q}}{p}}.

The asymptotics (
1.13
) and (
1.14
) then follow upon insertion of the convergence
{\upkappa}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)\to{(\upkappa_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
and
{\Upupsilon}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}(t)\to{(\Upupsilon_{\infty})}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
as
t\to 0
from Theorem
1.4
(i), as well as inserting the explicit form of
\mathring{g}_{ij}
and
t{\mathring{k}}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
.

To be completely precise, there is a caveat in that Theorem
1.4
(i) assumes the spatially harmonic condition (
1.39
) at
t=1
, but one may simply proceed via a time-independent vector field
\upxi^{j}
which brings us into this gauge, e.g. in a similar fashion to Lemma
4.3
(b) we take
\upxi^{j}
time-independent solving

t^{2}\mathring{g}^{ab}(1)\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\upxi^{j}=t^{2}\mathring{g}^{jk}(1)\left(\partial_{k}\tr\upeta(1)-2\partial_{\ell}{\upeta}_{{k}}^{\phantom{{k}}{\ell}}(1)\right).

One then transforms back, again using Lemma
4.3
; since
\upxi^{j}
is time-independent this does not change the structure of the desired asymptotics.
∎

Appendix A
Fourier decomposition and
\Psi
DOs

In Appendix
A
, we review the Fourier decomposition and Sobolev spaces
H^{s}
on
\mathbb{T}^{D}=[-\pi,\pi]^{D}
. We also review pseudodifferential operators on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
and their symbol calculus, and then mention some properties of the symbol
\mathcal{T}_{*}
defined in Definition
1.1
in Lemma
A.1
.

Definition A.1
.

Let
f:\mathbb{T}^{D}\to\mathbb{R}
be a sufficiently regular
17
17

17

From the standard Fourier theory,
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{D})
will be sufficient, however are eventually more interested in the Sobolev spaces
H^{s}
. As we also entertain
s<0
in some cases, one may even consider the case where
f
is simply a distribution.
function. The
standard Fourier decomposition
, or
Fourier series
,
(f_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
, is defined as follows. For
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{D})\in\mathbb{Z}^{D}
, we have

f_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{D}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{D}}f(x)e^{-i\lambda\cdot x}\,dx=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{D}}\int_{x\in[-\pi,\pi]^{D}}f(x)e^{-i\lambda_{k}x^{k}}\,dx^{1}\cdots dx^{D}.

(A.1)

A similar decomposition holds for tensors in
\mathbb{T}^{D}
, e.g. a
(1,1)
tensor
A={A}_{{i}}^{\phantom{{i}}{j}}
on
\mathbb{T}^{D}
